## Quarantine: nothing is worth keeping
The quarantine holds 53.77 GiB in 11 folders, and all of it is garbage:
- leftover app data and installers for already-uninstalled software;
- an old Codex cleanup folder;
- the broken Cursor install;
- a stale temp copy of a Claude config.

The two things worth knowing before you confirm:
- **Browser profiles:** the Brave and Opera profiles (bookmarks, any saved logins) are included. Neither browser is installed.
- **Old WSL tar:** it is the older of two backups, and neither has ever had an import test.

The script re-hashes the one surviving WSL backup and checks that WSL, Tailscale and Cursor work before it deletes anything. It writes a context record (names, sizes, file counts, READMEs, the old tar's SHA-256) to `D:\Backup\Quarantine-record\` first. It keeps `D:\Backup\WSL`, `Toolkit`, `dotfiles-*` and `GameSaves`. All 8 checks passed in the dry run.

## Rize, GitKrakenCLI, Python ghosts
- **Rize:** a 3.6 MB app plus 166 MB of data. The script runs its own uninstaller.
- **Python:** all 11 registrations can be cleaned properly. The bundle uninstaller and every cached MSI still exist. The script records the Machine PATH before and after to prove it isn't damaged. This also removes `C:\Windows\py.exe`.
- **GitKrakenCLI:** it is 365 MB, not logs, and it will come back. GitLens (installed in VS Code, Kiro and the WSL server) keeps downloading and updating `gk.exe`; the log shows an install last night. I found [no documented opt-out](https://forum.cursor.com/t/gitlens-mcp-server-auto-added-in-cursor-and-cannot-be-removed/159079). The script clears the folders now. They stay gone only if you uninstall GitLens, which is your call because extension lists sync between laptops.

## `packageManager` pin: what it is, and what I did
It is one line in a repo's `package.json`: `"packageManager": "pnpm@12.4.2"`. On the Dell `pnpm` is a Corepack shim, so inside that repo it runs *exactly* that version on every machine. It prevents the drift that hit the Acer (its global pnpm floated to 12.11.2). With pnpm 12 the lockfile also records the pnpm version with integrity hashes. Yes, it is useful for pnpm repos. It does nothing for Python repos.

| Repo | Result |
|---|---|
| `Learning-Tracker-Tool` | **Done, committed locally** (`51cc182`, branch `dell-latitude/pin-package-manager`, in an isolated worktree so your 2 untracked files are untouched). Frozen install exits 0, Next 16.1.6 runs, lint exits 0. **Not pushed.** |
| `portfolio` | **Blocked, reverted.** The lockfile on `origin/main` is already broken (duplicate key `@ai-sdk/mistral@3.0.39` at lines 201 and 207), so pnpm wanted to rewrite all of it (5,545 lines), which also floats dependency versions. Its old `onlyBuiltDependencies` block also no longer works under pnpm 12. Fixing this needs your OK. |
| `Assisto_website` | Not started. It has both `package-lock.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml`, so you must pick a manager first. |

pnpm 12 also ignores the old build-script allowlist keys, which is why the first repo needed `allowBuilds` set to `false` for sharp and unrs-resolver. That keeps its previous intent. `Learning-Tracker-Tool` is a **team repo** (a collaborator's fork and merges). Pushing the pin would force pnpm 12.4.2 on them. I haven't tested whether older pnpm reads the new lockfile section, so I'd hold the PR until you decide.

## Your commands, in this order

**1. Normal PowerShell.** This adopts the bat and fd configs the Acer promoted, and puts `~\.local\bin` back at the front of your user PATH. The dry run shows something moved it.
```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\40_Resources\CS\Concepts\New Laptop\Old Laptop Rebuild\Windows Toolkit\_scripts\20-apply-configs.ps1" -Machine dell
```

**2. Elevated PowerShell.** Rize, GitKrakenCLI folders, the 11 Python registrations.
```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File D:\WSL\ops\windows\09-remove-leftover-apps.ps1 -Apply
```

**3. Normal PowerShell, last.** It takes about 5 minutes, asks for `YES`, and frees about 54 GiB.
```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File D:\WSL\ops\windows\08-quarantine-purge.ps1 -Apply
```

Pin pnpm 12.4.2 through packageManager

Corepack now runs exactly pnpm 12.4.2 in this repo on every machine, and the
lockfile records the pnpm version with integrity hashes.

pnpm 12 no longer honors ignoredBuiltDependencies alone and refused to install
until each build script was decided, so allowBuilds keeps the existing intent
(sharp: false, unrs-resolver: false). The old key stays for older pnpm.

Verified: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile exits 0, next 16.1.6 starts, eslint exits 0.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
git --no-pager log --oneline -2; git status --short | wc -l; echo "upstream: $(git rev-parse --abbrev-ref @{u} 2>&1 | tail -1)"
echo; echo "######## portfolio"
R=~/projects/hub/portfolio; W2=~/projects/hub/.worktrees/portfolio-pin-package-manager; B=dell-latitude/pin-package-manager
git -C "$R" fetch origin --quiet && echo "origin/main = $(git -C $R rev-parse --short origin/main)"
git -C "$R" worktree add -b "$B" "$W2" origin/main 2>&1 | tail -1; cd "$W2"; git branch --unset-upstream 2>/dev/null
echo "branch: $(git branch --show-current)  lockfile: $(ls | grep -E "lock" | tr "\n" " ")"; grep -n "packageManager" package.json | head -2; echo "--- pnpm block in package.json:"; grep -n -A6 "\"pnpm\": {" package.json | head -10; echo "--- workspace file:"; cat pnpm-workspace.yaml 2>/dev/null || echo "(none)"; echo "pnpm before pin: $(pnpm --version | tail -1)"' 2>&1`

```
# 08-quarantine-purge.ps1  (Build 4b follow-up, 2026-10-17)  ASCII only. PowerShell 5.1 and 7 safe. No admin needed.
#   powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File D:\WSL\ops\windows\08-quarantine-purge.ps1            (dry run: checks, inventory, nothing deleted)
#   powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File D:\WSL\ops\windows\08-quarantine-purge.ps1 -Apply     (verifies, writes the context record, asks YES, deletes)
# Purpose: the Dell quarantine existed only so that, if something broke, there was context on what existed before. Everything is working and
# verified, so the quarantine folders are deleted. The CONTEXT is kept: an inventory record (names, sizes, file counts, READMEs, hashes) is written
# to D:\Backup\Quarantine-record\ before anything is deleted, and the key facts are copied into the vault by Claude afterwards.
# NEVER touched: D:\Backup\WSL (the only WSL backup), D:\Backup\Toolkit, D:\Backup\dotfiles-*, D:\Backup\GameSaves, D:\Backup\Quarantine itself (stays, empty).
# The only extra delete outside the quarantine: the malformed copy of the export manifest (D:\Backup\WSL\*.manifest.txt.malformed).
param([switch]$Apply)
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
$dry = -not $Apply
$logDir = 'D:\WSL\ops\windows\logs'
if (-not (Test-Path $logDir)) { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $logDir -Force | Out-Null }
$stamp = Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmmss'
$log = Join-Path $logDir ('quarantine-purge-' + $stamp + '.log')
try { Start-Transcript -Path (Join-Path $logDir ('quarantine-purge-' + $stamp + '.transcript.txt')) -Force | Out-Null } catch { }
function Log([string]$m) { $l = (Get-Date -Format 'HH:mm:ss') + ' ' + $m; Write-Host $l; Add-Content -Path $log -Value $l -Encoding ASCII }
Log ("quarantine purge. DryRun={0}  Log: {1}" -f $dry, $log)
$q = 'D:\Backup\Quarantine'
$recDir = 'D:\Backup\Quarantine-record'
$fail = 0

# ---- 1. checks that everything works and the one surviving WSL backup is intact
Log '== 1 checks (all must pass before anything is deleted)'
function Check([string]$name, [bool]$ok, [string]$detail) { if (-not $ok) { $script:fail++ }; Log ("  {0,-4} {1}  {2}" -f $(if ($ok) { 'OK' } else { 'FAIL' }), $name, $detail) }
$man = Get-ChildItem 'D:\Backup\WSL' -Filter 'ubuntu-*.tar.manifest.txt' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Sort-Object LastWriteTime -Descending | Select-Object -First 1
if (-not $man) { Check 'surviving WSL export manifest' $false 'none found in D:\Backup\WSL' }
else {
  $m = @{}; Get-Content $man.FullName | ForEach-Object { $p = $_.Split('=', 2); if ($p.Count -eq 2) { $m[$p[0]] = $p[1] } }
  $okSize = (Test-Path $m['file']) -and ((Get-Item $m['file']).Length -eq [int64]$m['bytes'])
  Check 'surviving WSL export size matches manifest' $okSize ("{0} ({1:N2} GiB)" -f $m['file'], ([int64]$m['bytes'] / 1GB))
  if ($okSize) {
    $sw = [Diagnostics.Stopwatch]::StartNew(); $h = (Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 -Path $m['file']).Hash.ToLower()
    Check 'surviving WSL export SHA-256 matches manifest' ($h -eq $m['sha256']) ("{0:N1} min" -f $sw.Elapsed.TotalMinutes)
  }
}
$sys = ((wsl.exe -d Ubuntu --exec systemctl is-system-running 2>$null) -join '').Trim()
Check 'WSL Ubuntu systemd running' ($sys -eq 'running') $sys
$ts = ((wsl.exe -d Ubuntu --exec tailscale status 2>$null) -join ' ')
Check 'Tailscale lists dell-wsl' ($ts -match 'dell-wsl') ''
$cur = Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'Programs\cursor\Cursor.exe'
Check 'Cursor installed' (Test-Path $cur) $(if (Test-Path $cur) { (Get-Item $cur).VersionInfo.ProductVersion } else { 'missing' })
Check 'OpenClaw is gone' (-not (Get-Command openclaw -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) ''
Check 'toolkit lock present' (Test-Path 'D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\40_Resources\CS\Concepts\New Laptop\Old Laptop Rebuild\Windows Toolkit\toolkit.lock.json') ''
Check 'no Cursor update in progress' (@(Get-Process -Name inno_updater -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue).Count -eq 0) ''
if ($fail -gt 0) { Log ("STOP: {0} check(s) failed. Nothing was deleted." -f $fail); Log "DONE. Log: $log"; try { Stop-Transcript | Out-Null } catch { }; exit 2 }

# ---- 2. inventory
Log '== 2 inventory of what would be deleted'
$items = @(Get-ChildItem $q -Directory -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
$rec = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
[void]$rec.Add("Quarantine context record, written " + (Get-Date -Format 's') + " by 08-quarantine-purge.ps1 on " + $env:COMPUTERNAME)
[void]$rec.Add("Purpose: what existed in D:\Backup\Quarantine before it was deleted. The files are gone; this list is the context.")
$total = 0
foreach ($d in $items) {
  $files = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $d.FullName -Recurse -Force -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
  $bytes = ($files | Measure-Object Length -Sum).Sum; if (-not $bytes) { $bytes = 0 }
  $total += $bytes
  Log ("  {0,-48} {1,8:N2} GiB {2,8:N0} files" -f $d.Name, ($bytes / 1GB), $files.Count)
  [void]$rec.Add(""); [void]$rec.Add(("== {0}  {1:N2} GiB  {2} files" -f $d.Name, ($bytes / 1GB), $files.Count))
  foreach ($c in (Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $d.FullName -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) {
    $cf = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $c.FullName -Recurse -Force -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue); $cb = ($cf | Measure-Object Length -Sum).Sum; if (-not $cb) { $cb = 0 }
    [void]$rec.Add(("   {0}  {1:N3} GiB  {2} files" -f $c.Name, ($cb / 1GB), $cf.Count))
  }
  foreach ($r in (Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $d.FullName -Force -File -Filter 'README*' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) { [void]$rec.Add("   README: " + ((Get-Content -LiteralPath $r.FullName -TotalCount 6) -join ' | ')) }
  foreach ($r in (Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $d.FullName -Force -File -Filter 'undo.txt' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) { [void]$rec.Add("   undo.txt lines: " + @(Get-Content -LiteralPath $r.FullName).Count + " (restore steps for the move that created this folder)") }
}
[void]$rec.Add(""); [void]$rec.Add(("TOTAL {0:N2} GiB in {1} folders" -f ($total / 1GB), $items.Count))
[void]$rec.Add("Old WSL tar ([REDACTED]): written 2026-01-15, 39,454,945,280 bytes, 455,976 entries, SHA-256 [REDACTED]; superseded by D:\Backup\WSL\ubuntu-20261014-233219.tar (no import test was ever run on either).")
$malformed = Get-ChildItem 'D:\Backup\WSL' -Filter '*.manifest.txt.malformed' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
Log ("  malformed manifest copy to delete: {0}" -f $(if ($malformed) { ($malformed | ForEach-Object Name) -join ', ' } else { 'none' }))
Log ("  TOTAL {0:N2} GiB" -f ($total / 1GB))
Log '  NOTE: c-deep-clean-review includes the Brave and Opera browser profiles (bookmarks and any saved logins; neither browser is installed), JetBrains settings, Outlook/Office caches, Medal, WPS and old installers.'
Log '  NOTE: keep list (not touched): D:\Backup\WSL, D:\Backup\Toolkit, D:\Backup\dotfiles-*, D:\Backup\GameSaves.'

if ($dry) { Log 'DRY RUN: nothing deleted. Re-run with -Apply.'; Log "DONE. Log: $log"; try { Stop-Transcript | Out-Null } catch { }; exit 0 }

# ---- 3. confirm, record, delete
$a = Read-Host ("Delete {0:N2} GiB in {1} quarantine folders? The context record is written first. Type YES to continue" -f ($total / 1GB), $items.Count)
if ($a -ne 'YES') { Log 'Not confirmed. Nothing deleted.'; Log "DONE. Log: $log"; try { Stop-Transcript | Out-Null } catch { }; exit 0 }
if (-not (Test-Path $recDir)) { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $recDir -Force | Out-Null }
$recFile = Join-Path $recDir ('quarantine-record-' + (Get-Date -Format 'yyyy-MM-dd') + '.txt')
[IO.File]::WriteAllLines($recFile, [string[]]$rec, (New-Object Text.ASCIIEncoding))
Log ("context record written: {0}" -f $recFile)
$before = (Get-PSDrive D).Free
foreach ($d in $items) {
  Log ("  deleting {0}" -f $d.Name)
  & cmd.exe /c ('rd /s /q "\\?\' + $d.FullName + '"') 2>&1 | Out-Null
  if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $d.FullName) { Log ("    STILL THERE after rd: {0} (locked or protected; re-run or delete by hand)" -f $d.Name) } else { Log '    gone' }
}
foreach ($f in $malformed) { Remove-Item -LiteralPath $f.FullName -Force; Log ("  deleted {0}" -f $f.Name) }
$after = (Get-PSDrive D).Free
Log ("D: free {0:N1} -> {1:N1} GiB (freed {2:N2} GiB)" -f ($before / 1GB), ($after / 1GB), (($after - $before) / 1GB))
Log ("remaining in {0}: {1}" -f $q, @(Get-ChildItem $q -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue).Count)
Log "DONE. Log: $log"
try { Stop-Transcript | Out-Null } catch { }

```
- `PowerShell` -- `{"command":"$f=\u0027D:\\WSL\\ops\\windows\\08-quarantine-purge.ps1\u0027; \"non-ASCII: \" + @([IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($f) | ? {$_ -gt 127}).Count; $e=$null; $null=[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile($f,[ref]$null,[ref]$e); \"parse errors: \" + @($e).Count\npowershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $f; \"exit=$LASTEXITCODE\"; \"quarantine still intact: \" + @(Get-ChildItem D:\\Backup\\Quarantine -Force).Count + \" folders; record dir exists: \" + (Test-Path D:\\Backup\\Quarantine-record)","description":"Dry-run the quarantine purge script","timeout":900000}`
- `Write` -- `D:\WSL\ops\windows\09-remove-leftover-apps.ps1`
```
# 09-remove-leftover-apps.ps1  (Build 4b follow-up, 2026-10-17)  ASCII only. PowerShell 5.1 and 7 safe.
#   powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File D:\WSL\ops\windows\09-remove-leftover-apps.ps1            (dry run: lists what exists, works in a normal shell)
#   ELEVATED PowerShell:  ... 09-remove-leftover-apps.ps1 -Apply                                       (removes it; needs admin because 10 Python entries are per-machine MSIs)
# Removes (all approved by Anant on 2026-10-17):
#   1. Rize: Squirrel uninstall, then its folders, its (already disabled) startup entries.
#   2. GitKrakenCLI leftovers: %LOCALAPPDATA%\GitKrakenCLI and %LOCALAPPDATA%\gk (logs and an install lock; no program is installed).
#   3. The 11 stale Python 3.13.5 uninstall registrations (files are gone since Build 4): the bundle's own uninstaller first, then msiexec /x for stragglers.
# Machine PATH is recorded (type, length, SHA-256) before and after the Python step to prove the uninstall did not damage it.
param([switch]$Apply)
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
$dry = -not $Apply
$logDir = 'D:\WSL\ops\windows\logs'
if (-not (Test-Path $logDir)) { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $logDir -Force | Out-Null }
$stamp = Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmmss'
$log = Join-Path $logDir ('remove-leftover-apps-' + $stamp + '.log')
try { Start-Transcript -Path (Join-Path $logDir ('remove-leftover-apps-' + $stamp + '.transcript.txt')) -Force | Out-Null } catch { }
function Log([string]$m) { $l = (Get-Date -Format 'HH:mm:ss') + ' ' + $m; Write-Host $l; Add-Content -Path $log -Value $l -Encoding ASCII }
Log ("remove leftover apps. DryRun={0}  Log: {1}" -f $dry, $log)
$isAdmin = ([Security.Principal.WindowsPrincipal][Security.Principal.WindowsIdentity]::GetCurrent()).IsInRole([Security.Principal.WindowsBuiltInRole]::Administrator)
Log ("  elevated: {0}" -f $isAdmin)
if ($Apply -and -not $isAdmin) { Log 'STOP: -Apply needs an elevated PowerShell (the Python MSIs are per-machine).'; Log "DONE. Log: $log"; try { Stop-Transcript | Out-Null } catch { }; exit 2 }
function Size-MB([string]$p) { if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $p)) { return 'absent' }; $s = (Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $p -Recurse -Force -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Measure-Object Length -Sum).Sum; if (-not $s) { $s = 0 }; return ('{0:N1} MB' -f ($s / 1MB)) }
function Remove-Tree([string]$p) { if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $p) { & cmd.exe /c ('rd /s /q "\\?\' + $p + '"') 2>&1 | Out-Null; if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $p) { Log ("    STILL THERE: {0}" -f $p) } else { Log ("    removed {0}" -f $p) } } }

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- 1 Rize
Log '== 1 Rize'
$rizeLocal = Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'Rize'; $rizeRoam = Join-Path $env:APPDATA 'Rize'
$runKey = 'HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run'; $saKey = 'HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Explorer\StartupApproved\Run'; $rn = 'com.squirrel.Rize.Rize'
Log ("  {0}  {1}" -f $rizeLocal, (Size-MB $rizeLocal)); Log ("  {0}  {1}" -f $rizeRoam, (Size-MB $rizeRoam))
Log ("  Run value present: {0}   StartupApproved value present: {1}" -f [bool](Get-ItemProperty $runKey -Name $rn -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue), [bool](Get-ItemProperty $saKey -Name $rn -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue))
$rp = @(Get-Process -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object { $_.Name -match '^rize' })
if ($rp.Count -gt 0) { Log ("  BLOCKED: Rize is running ({0} process). Quit it from its tray icon and re-run." -f $rp.Count) }
elseif ($dry) { Log '  [dry run] would run Update.exe --uninstall -s, delete both folders and both startup values' }
else {
  $u = Join-Path $rizeLocal 'Update.exe'
  if (Test-Path $u) { $p = Start-Process -FilePath $u -ArgumentList '--uninstall', '-s' -Wait -PassThru; Log ("  Squirrel uninstall exit code {0}" -f $p.ExitCode) }
  Remove-Tree $rizeLocal; Remove-Tree $rizeRoam
  foreach ($k in $runKey, $saKey) { if (Get-ItemProperty $k -Name $rn -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue) { Remove-ItemProperty -Path $k -Name $rn; Log ("  removed value {0} from {1}" -f $rn, $k) } }
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- 2 GitKrakenCLI
Log '== 2 GitKrakenCLI leftovers'
$gk = @((Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'GitKrakenCLI'), (Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'gk'))
foreach ($g in $gk) { Log ("  {0}  {1}" -f $g, (Size-MB $g)) }
$gp = @(Get-Process -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object { $_.Name -match '^(gk|gitkraken)' })
if ($gp.Count -gt 0) { Log ("  BLOCKED: {0} GitKraken process running; close it and re-run." -f $gp.Count) }
elseif ($dry) { Log '  [dry run] would delete both folders' } else { foreach ($g in $gk) { Remove-Tree $g } }

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- 3 Python registrations
Log '== 3 stale Python 3.13.5 registrations'
$msi = [ordered]@{
  '{[REDACTED]}' = 'Documentation'; '{[REDACTED]}' = 'pip Bootstrap'; '{[REDACTED]}' = 'Executables'
  '{[REDACTED]}' = 'Core Interpreter'; '{[REDACTED]}' = 'Test Suite'; '{[REDACTED]}' = 'Tcl/Tk Support'
  '{[REDACTED]}' = 'Development Libraries'; '{[REDACTED]}' = 'Standard Library'; '{[REDACTED]}' = 'Add to Path'
  '{[REDACTED]}' = 'Python Launcher'
}
$bundleGuid = '{[REDACTED]}'
$uk = @('HKLM:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall', 'HKLM:\Software\WOW6432Node\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall')
function Count-Registered { $n = 0; foreach ($g in $msi.Keys) { foreach ($k in $uk) { if (Test-Path (Join-Path $k $g)) { $n++; break } } }; if (Test-Path ('HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\' + $bundleGuid)) { $n++ }; return $n }
function Machine-Path { $k = [Microsoft.Win32.Registry]::LocalMachine.OpenSubKey('SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\Session Manager\Environment'); $v = [string]$k.GetValue('Path', '', [Microsoft.Win32.RegistryValueOptions]::DoNotExpandEnvironmentNames); $t = $k.GetValueKind('Path'); $h = [BitConverter]::ToString([Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create().ComputeHash([Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetBytes($v))).Replace('-', '').Substring(0, 16); return ('type={0} length={1} sha256={2}' -f $t, $v.Length, $h) }
$before = Count-Registered
Log ("  registered now: {0} of 11 (10 MSI components + 1 bundle)" -f $before)
$bundle = Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA ('Package Cache\' + $bundleGuid + '\python-3.13.5-amd64.exe')
Log ("  bundle uninstaller present: {0}   C:\Python313 present: {1}   C:\Windows\py.exe present: {2}" -f (Test-Path $bundle), (Test-Path 'C:\Python313'), (Test-Path 'C:\Windows\py.exe'))
Log ("  Machine PATH before: {0}" -f (Machine-Path))
if ($dry) { Log '  [dry run] would run the bundle uninstaller (/uninstall /quiet /norestart), then msiexec /x for anything still registered, then re-count' }
else {
  if (Test-Path $bundle) { $p = Start-Process -FilePath $bundle -ArgumentList '/uninstall', '/quiet', '/norestart' -Wait -PassThru; Log ("  bundle uninstall exit code {0} (0 ok, 3010 reboot needed)" -f $p.ExitCode) } else { Log '  bundle uninstaller missing; going straight to msiexec' }
  foreach ($g in $msi.Keys) {
    $still = $false; foreach ($k in $uk) { if (Test-Path (Join-Path $k $g)) { $still = $true } }
    if ($still) { $p = Start-Process -FilePath 'msiexec.exe' -ArgumentList '/x', $g, '/qn', '/norestart' -Wait -PassThru; Log ("  msiexec /x {0} ({1}) exit code {2} (1605 or 1614 = already gone, 3010 = reboot)" -f $g, $msi[$g], $p.ExitCode) }
  }
  $bk = 'HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\' + $bundleGuid
  if (Test-Path $bk) { Log '  bundle registration still present after uninstall; removing its Uninstall key (registration only, no files)'; Remove-Item -Path $bk -Recurse -Force }
  Log ("  Machine PATH after:  {0}" -f (Machine-Path))
  Log ("  registered now: {0} of 11" -f (Count-Registered))
  Log ("  C:\Windows\py.exe present: {0}" -f (Test-Path 'C:\Windows\py.exe'))
  $w = (winget list --disable-interactivity 2>$null | Select-String -Pattern 'Python 3\.13\.5|Python Launcher' | Measure-Object).Count
  Log ("  winget list still shows {0} Python 3.13.5 / Launcher row(s)" -f $w)
}
if ($dry) { Log 'DRY RUN: nothing changed.' }
Log "DONE. Log: $log"
try { Stop-Transcript | Out-Null } catch { }

# Old Laptop Rebuild - Acer Visit Findings (2026-10-15)

## One-Line Answer
==On 2026-10-15 the Acer applied everything the Dell had built: the WSL golden toolkit (Build 5b), Parts 0 to 3 of the Windows toolkit (Build 4) and all Build 2 SSH checks, plus a WSL platform update to 3.0.3.0; both laptops now run the same configs and nearly the same tool versions, the independent SSH host-key check matched, and the one real risk found is four internship commits that exist only on the Acer.==

## Scope and sources
Everything in the Acer sections is **Acer-reported** by the Claude and user sessions that ran on `gupta-builds` between 16:41 and about 19:00 CDT on 2026-10-15; the Dell session could read their notes and result files but cannot see the Acer's disk. Where the Dell could check a claim (the host key, the Windows catalogs, the config hashes) it did, and that is marked. This note replaces three instruction notes that existed only to drive that visit: the Acer WSL handoff (Build 5b), the Windows run sheet and the Build 2 Acer handoff. Their results are kept here; their step lists are condensed into "How to repeat this on a machine" at the bottom. Raw evidence is in the vault: `Windows Toolkit/results/GUPTA-BUILDS/*.json`, `Windows Toolkit/catalog/Tool Catalog - GUPTA-BUILDS.md`.

## Part 1: WSL toolkit sync (Build 5b on the Acer)
Steps run in order with backups to `~/.config/.toolkit-backups/`: health baseline (`36-wsl-check.sh`), tmux server closed, `37-toolkit-apply.sh`, `38-toolkit-upgrade.sh`, TPM plugin install (all six plugins), then the Acer-only fixes. Found and fixed beyond the planned fix table:
- **The cause of the pnpm float:** the Acer's own weekly cleanup deleted `~/.cache/node`, which holds Corepack's pnpm pin; that is how pnpm reached 12.11.2. The same mistake had happened on the Dell in Build 5 (see that note). Rule: nothing may delete `~/.cache/node` on any machine.
- **Not covered by `38-toolkit-upgrade.sh`, done by hand:** delta, atuin (release tarballs with SHA-256 checked against GitHub digests), uv (`uv self update`), codex (`npm i -g`), ruff (`uv tool`), shellcheck and shfmt (checksum checked). Old binaries are in `~/.local/bin/.prev`.
- **Item 7 (`.bashrc` hardening):** every JARVIS hook guarded with `command -v`, `~/.local/bin` added to PATH only if absent, duplicate nvm load removed, `bash -n` passes, new-shell startup 0.57 s.
- **`bat cache --build`** was needed once because the first run reported an incompatible cache from the older bat.
- **tmux-handlr ntfy topic:** `~/.config/tmux-handlr/ntfy.env` exists (mode 600); the topic value was not read or recorded.
- **Do not run on the Acer:** `31-wsl-sudo-step.sh` (it also runs `systemctl disable --now ollama`, which fails there because no WSL Ollama exists, and with `set -e` it would stop before its checks) and `30-wsl-config-parity.sh` (it would overwrite the golden configs with the old snapshot). For socat: `sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y socat bubblewrap`.
- **Golden-set notes for the Dell:** `36-wsl-check.sh` had the Dell's vault path and the old swap file name hard-coded and now detects both; `37` does not set `core.autocrlf`, so `input` was set by hand; the shared bat and fd configs were reconstructions (promoted, below).

## Part 2: Windows toolkit on the Acer (Build 4, Parts 0 to 3)
| Part | Result |
|---|---|
| 0 (read-only) | preflight 47 manifest entries, 5 planned actions; the dry run would upgrade GitHub CLI, install pnpm, a uv-managed Python and mcp-remote; Node reported MANUAL because the manifest names `OpenJS.NodeJS.22` and the Acer is on 24.19.0 |
| 1 (UAC) | `INSTALLED=3 MANUAL=1 OK=30 UPGRADED=1`: pnpm 12.11.2, uv-managed Python 3.13.15, mcp-remote 0.2.1; GitHub CLI 2.101.0 to 2.102.0; Git was already 2.55.0 |
| 2 (apply) | starship and git ignore unchanged (hashes equal), delta include written, both profile blocks written, Terminal merged (Acer profiles kept), 9 git settings changed, user PATH order fixed, two environment variables set. Backups in `D:\Backup\Toolkit\20261015-170556` (restore with `-RestoreFrom`) |
| 3 (verify, catalog) | `99-verify.ps1` **0 FAIL**; every config, git, profile, Terminal, font, environment and startup check PASS; `40-build-catalog.ps1` PASS, 41 manifest tools |
Promotion: the Acer's real `bat` and `fd` configs were copied byte for byte into `Windows Toolkit/configs/` (SHA-256 `F613417D...` and `6A549EAF...`); the shared copies had been reconstructions. `toolkit.json` was not edited, so the Dell lock stays valid. **Dell action, done 2026-10-17:** `20-apply-configs.ps1` ran on the Dell (bat and fd written, hashes match, and the Dell's user PATH was put back in the right order, `~\.local\bin` first).

## Part 3: Build 2 SSH checks from the Acer
| Check | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Tailscale placement | PASS | Windows 1.104.1 connected as `gupta-builds` (`100.84.91.11`); inside the Acer WSL there is no `tailscale` binary, no package, `tailscaled` not active |
| Route | PASS | direct `via 10.5.8.253:41641`, 4 to 5 ms (13 ms in a later sample); control plane shows `dell-wsl` online with relay `ord` |
| Remote command | PASS | plain `ssh.exe anant_gupta@dell-wsl` ran `hostname` (`DESKTOP-3VBG0JH`) and `uptime`; Tailscale SSH asked for its browser "check" authentication once |
| **Host key** | **PASS (MATCH), closed 2026-10-15** | Neither side's `tailscale status --json` carries `SSH_HostKeys` for the Dell (checked on both). The user ran `71-hostkey-fingerprint.sh` on the Dell (and an equivalent openssl one-liner): `SHA256:[REDACTED]`, equal to the key the Acer accepted; `known_hosts` and `ssh-keyscan` agree. `ssh-keygen -lf` cannot read Tailscale's PKCS8 key file, hence the script |
| `sync-check` over SSH | PASS | identical to the Dell's local run: second-brain-claudekit `main` 0/0, dirty 1; internship-research-loop `dell-latitude/deadline-quota-and-reseed-fixes` 6 ahead / 5 behind `origin/master`, dirty 25. The `node`, `pnpm`, `uv` lines read `not-installed` over a non-interactive shell (nvm and `~/.local/bin` are set in the interactive `.bashrc`); the repo lines are the check |
| Large transfer | measured, slow | 17,039,678-byte `git bundle` streamed in 2.38 s (6.8 MiB/s); a 512 MiB stream took 197 s, **2.5 MiB/s**, on a direct 4 to 13 ms path. Cause not established (candidates: one SSH stream through the Dell's userspace Tailscale, `tailscale0` MTU 1280, mirrored networking). No setting changed. The largest repo history on the Dell is `jan` at 1.5 GB: test before cloning it over this link |
| Acer WSL route | PASS | `dell-wsl` resolves to `100.94.129.113` in the Acer WSL, `tcp/22` reachable, `ssh` with a temporary known-hosts file holding only the verified key ran `hostname` |
| Remote-SSH execution location | not re-tested | the 2026-10-12 evidence stands (extension host on the Dell WSL server); needs a GUI VS Code window |
Gotcha: Tailscale SSH "check" mode asks for a browser confirmation after a period of inactivity; a non-interactive run prints a `login.tailscale.com/a/...` URL and waits.

## Acer WSL disk (inventory for the planned discussion, read-only)
`/` is 39 GiB (72 percent used, 11 GiB free) with `ext4.vhdx` 28.9 GiB on D:. Largest items: `~/.cache` 7.7 GB (uv 7.5 GB, regenerable), `.vscode-server` 4.1 GB, `miniconda3` 3.8 GB, `.codex` 2.1 GB, `.local` 1.8 GB, `.rustup` 1.5 GB, `.npm` 0.9 GB, `.nvm` 0.7 GB, `.cargo` 0.5 GB, `projects` 252 MB plus `scratch/gpu-smoke-test` 5.3 GB. The Acer has no `wsl --export` backup yet: take one before any resize. Planned discussion (after the builds): what the Acer's WSL is for now that the Dell hosts the code, what to do with the two clones and `gpu-smoke-test`, resize versus rebuild, and which Windows pieces make Remote-SSH smoother.

## Errors and root causes (Acer)
| Error | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Every Windows toolkit script failed with "The term 'Get-FileHash' is not recognized" | started from a shell that inherited PowerShell 7's `PSModulePath`, so Windows PowerShell 5.1 loaded PowerShell 7's utility module first | `$env:PSModulePath = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('PSModulePath','Machine')` before starting; a normal 5.1 window does not need it. Worth a guard in `_lib.ps1` |
| Part 3 failed with "'\_scripts\99-verify.ps1' does not exist" | `$TK` was undefined in the new terminal window | define `$V` and `$TK` in every new window |
| pnpm floated to 12.11.2 | the Acer's weekly cleanup deleted `~/.cache/node` | deletion removed; rule recorded |
| `70-acer-wsl-update.ps1` printed a blank `systemd:` line | it checks a few seconds after the restart | cosmetic; check `systemctl is-system-running` after a minute (it was `running`) |
| `ssh-keygen -lf` on the Tailscale host key: "is not a key file" | Tailscale stores a plain PKCS8 PEM | `71-hostkey-fingerprint.sh` derives the public key with openssl |
| Docker Desktop closed by the WSL update | `wsl --update` / shutdown stops all distros | the user reopens it; its engine was not running at the check |

2. Discussion: the role of WSL on the Acer (disk 72 percent used, no export backup).
3. Remote-SSH execution-location re-check in a GUI window; clone retirement approvals.
4. Why the SSH stream is only 2.5 MiB/s (measure before cloning large histories).
5. Reopen Docker Desktop on the Acer.
6. Move the Acer's Node 24.19.0 to the lock's 24.20.0 only if wanted (manifest policy is manual).

## Growth rules
| Standing rule | Check |
|---|---|
| Nothing deletes `~/.cache/node` on any machine (it holds the Corepack pin) | `grep -rn "cache/node" ~/tools ~/.local/bin ~/.config/systemd 2>/dev/null` must show only comments |
| Size a repo's transfer cost without cloning | `ssh <host> 'cd <repo> && git bundle create - --all' \| wc -c` |
| Start Windows toolkit scripts from a clean 5.1 environment | `$env:PSModulePath = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('PSModulePath','Machine')` |
| Secrets reach only the tools that need them | from `env -i bash -ic 'env \| grep -c _API_KEY'` expect 0 outside the AI-CLI wrappers |
| A WSL update closes Docker Desktop | reopen it and check `docker version` |

## How to repeat this on a machine (condensed from the retired instruction notes)
**Windows side** (normal PowerShell, scripts in `Windows Toolkit/_scripts`, vault root differs per machine): set `$V` (vault root) and `$TK` (toolkit folder); run `00-preflight.ps1`, `10-install-toolkit.ps1 -DryRun`, `20-apply-configs.ps1 -DryRun` and `99-verify.ps1` first (read-only); then `10-install-toolkit.ps1` (UAC prompts), `20-apply-configs.ps1`, a fresh window, `99-verify.ps1`, `40-build-catalog.ps1`. Pass `-Machine acer` or `-Machine dell` if the vault path is unusual. Never run `11-node-lts-swap.ps1` or `04-admin-remove.ps1` on the Acer. Never pass `-WriteLock` on the Acer.
**WSL side** (scripts in `WSL Parity/_scripts`): `36-wsl-check.sh`, close tmux, `37-toolkit-apply.sh --dry-run` then real, `38-toolkit-upgrade.sh --dry-run` then real, `prefix + I` once in tmux. Details of each config: [[WSL Toolkit Config Reference]].
**SSH checks:** `tailscale.exe ping dell-wsl`; plain `ssh.exe anant_gupta@dell-wsl`; read the host key on the Dell with `71-hostkey-fingerprint.sh` (sudo); `ssh dell-wsl '~/tools/sync-check'`.

# helper for vault note edits. dot-source:  . "<scratchpad>\notehelp.ps1"
$script:ORL = 'D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\40_Resources\CS\Concepts\New Laptop\Old Laptop Rebuild'
$script:NL  = 'D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\40_Resources\CS\Concepts\New Laptop'
$script:Utf8 = New-Object Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
$script:EmDash = [string][char]0x2014
function Read-Note([string]$p) { [IO.File]::ReadAllText($p, [Text.Encoding]::UTF8) }
function Write-Note([string]$p, [string]$t) { [IO.File]::WriteAllText($p, $t.Replace("`r`n", "`n"), $script:Utf8) }
# Replace exactly one occurrence of each old string; abort the whole file if any is not unique.
function Patch-Note([string]$path, $pairs) {
  $t = Read-Note $path; $bad = 0
  foreach ($p in $pairs) { $n = ([regex]::Matches($t, [regex]::Escape($p[0]))).Count; if ($n -ne 1) { Write-Host ("  NOT UNIQUE ({0}x) in {1}: {2}" -f $n, (Split-Path $path -Leaf), $p[0].Substring(0, [Math]::Min(80, $p[0].Length))); $bad++ } }
  if ($bad -gt 0) { Write-Host "  ABORTED $(Split-Path $path -Leaf): $bad pattern problem(s), file unchanged"; return $false }
  foreach ($p in $pairs) { $t = $t.Replace($p[0], $p[1]) }
  Write-Note $path $t; Write-Host ("  patched {0} ({1} edits)" -f (Split-Path $path -Leaf), @($pairs).Count); return $true
}
function Set-Frontmatter([string]$path, [hashtable]$kv) {
  $t = Read-Note $path
  if (-not $t.StartsWith("---`n")) { Write-Host "  no frontmatter in $path"; return }
  $end = $t.IndexOf("`n---`n", 4); $fm = $t.Substring(4, $end - 4); $rest = $t.Substring($end + 5)
  foreach ($k in $kv.Keys) {
    $line = "$k`: $($kv[$k])"
    if ($fm -match "(?m)^$k`:") { $fm = [regex]::Replace($fm, "(?m)^$k`:.*$", [System.Text.RegularExpressions.MatchEvaluator]{ param($m) $line }) } else { $fm = $fm + "`n" + $line }
  }
  Write-Note $path ("---`n" + $fm + "`n---`n" + $rest)
}
# Replace the whole related: list of the frontmatter (list lines start with '  - ')
function Set-Related([string]$path, [string[]]$links) {
  $t = Read-Note $path; $end = $t.IndexOf("`n---`n", 4); $fm = $t.Substring(4, $end - 4); $rest = $t.Substring($end + 5)
  $new = "related:`n" + (($links | % { '  - "[[' + $_ + ']]"' }) -join "`n")
  if ($fm -match '(?ms)^related:\r?\n(?:  - .*\r?\n?)+') { $fm = [regex]::Replace($fm, '(?ms)^related:\r?\n(?:  - .*(?:\r?\n|$))+', ($new + "`n")) } else { $fm = $fm.TrimEnd() + "`n" + $new }
  Write-Note $path ("---`n" + $fm.TrimEnd() + "`n---`n" + $rest)
}
# Insert text before the first occurrence of a marker line
function Insert-Before([string]$path, [string]$marker, [string]$text) {
  $t = Read-Note $path; $i = $t.IndexOf($marker); if ($i -lt 0) { Write-Host "  marker not found: $marker"; return $false }
  if (([regex]::Matches($t, [regex]::Escape($marker))).Count -ne 1) { Write-Host "  marker not unique: $marker"; return $false }
  Write-Note $path ($t.Substring(0, $i) + $text.Replace("`r`n", "`n") + $t.Substring($i)); Write-Host ("  inserted block in {0}" -f (Split-Path $path -Leaf)); return $true
}
function Check-Note([string]$path) {
  $t = Read-Note $path
  '{0,-70} chars={1,7} emdash={2} bom={3}' -f (Split-Path $path -Leaf), $t.Length, ([regex]::Matches($t, $script:EmDash)).Count, ([IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($path)[0] -eq 0xEF)
}

$lines = $t -split \"`n\"; $idx = @(); for ($i = 0; $i -lt $lines.Count; $i++) { if ($lines[$i].StartsWith($prefix)) { $idx += $i } }\n  if ($idx.Count -ne 1) { Write-Host (\"  LINE NOT UNIQUE ({0}x): {1}\" -f $idx.Count, $prefix.Substring(0, [Math]::Min(70, $prefix.Length))); return $false }\n  $lines[$idx[0]] = $newLine; Write-Note $path ($lines -join \"`n\"); return $true\n}\n# Replace everything from $startMarker (inclusive) up to $endMarker (exclusive) with $text\nfunction Replace-Block([string]$path, [string]$startMarker, [string]$endMarker, [string]$text) {\n  $t = Read-Note $path; $a = $t.IndexOf($startMarker); $b = $t.IndexOf($endMarker, [Math]::Max($a, 0) + 1)\n  if ($a -lt 0 -or $b -lt 0) { Write-Host \"  block markers not found: $startMarker -\u003e $endMarker\"; return $false }\n  if (([regex]::Matches($t, [regex]::Escape($startMarker))).Count -ne 1) { Write-Host \"  start marker not unique\"; return $true\n}\n\u0027@\nif(-not ([IO.File]::ReadAllText($h)).Contains(\u0027function Replace-Line\u0027)){ [IO.File]::AppendAllText($h,$add,(New-Object Text.UTF8Encoding($false))) }\n. $h\n$f=\"$ORL\\Runs\\Build 2 VS Code and Two-Laptop\\Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 VS Code and Two-Laptop Findings.md\"\nCopy-Item $f \"D:\\Cache\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Anant-Gupta\\[REDACTED]\\scratchpad\\b2.orig.md\" -Force\n$lines=(Read-Note $f) -split \"`n\"; - `PowerShell` -- `{"command":". \u0027D:\\Cache\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Anant-Gupta\\[REDACTED]\\scratchpad\\notehelp.ps1\u0027\n$f3=\"$ORL\\Runs\\Build 3 Windows Cleanup\\Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 3 Windows Cleanup Findings.md\"\n$f3b=\"$ORL\\Runs\\Build 3 Windows Cleanup\\Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 3b C Drive Snapshot Structure and Watch.md\"\nCopy-Item $f3 \"D:\\Cache\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Anant-Gupta\\[REDACTED]\\scratchpad\\b3.orig.md\" -Force\nCopy-Item $f3b \"D:\\Cache\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Anant-Gupta\\[REDACTED]\\scratchpad\\b3b.orig.md\" -Force\n$o3=@\u0027\n## Verified outcome (checked 2026-10-17)\n**Purpose.** Free the Dell\u0027s C: drive, stop the things that refill it, give D: a clean layout, and decide the pagefile, Google Drive and startup questions. This note is the **audit and plan** (2026-10-11 to 2026-10-12, all read-only until the scripts ran); the execution record, the structure contract and the watch design are in [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 3b C Drive Snapshot Structure and Watch]].\n**What ran.** Scripts `01` to `08` in `D:\\WSL\\ops\\windows` (now archived or retired), run by the user in an elevated shell with dry runs first: Logi Options+, VC++ 2010/2012 and .NET 9 removed; Microsoft 365, OneDrive, WinRAR, Respondus, Dell Optimizer, MongoDB Shell, Teams, LinkedIn, Netflix and HP Printer Control removed; component store cleaned (about 22 GB); caches redirected through user environment variables; Miniconda, opam, hermes and the codex packages moved to D: behind junctions; Ollama reinstalled on `D:\\Apps`; Syncthing moved to `D:\\Apps\\Syncthing`; the pagefile fixed; two scheduled tasks registered.\n**Result.** C: free went from **39.5 GiB to 136.4 GiB** (3b measurement), peaked at 145.4 GiB on 2026-10-13 and read **120.8 GiB on 2026-10-17**. The recent drop is almost certainly the system-managed pagefile growing from about 16 GB (the 3b snapshot) to 31 GB (30,816 MB allocated, 6.1 GB in use on 2026-10-17) after heavy memory use; a reboot should return about 15 GiB. That is an inference to confirm, not a measurement.\n\n| Item | Final state (verified 2026-10-17) |\n|---|---|\n| Pagefile | `C:\\pagefile.sys` only, system managed 16,384 to 32,768 MB (registry `PagingFiles`). The earlier plans (v1: C: 4 GB + D: 32 GB; v2: C: 16 GB + D: 32 GB in two stages) were dropped: no D: pagefile exists and the BitLocker auto-unlock (external `.BEK`) hypothesis for why D: failed at boot was **never tested** |\n| Cache redirects | user variables set and verified: `UV_CACHE_DIR=D:\\Cache\\uv`, `PIP_CACHE_DIR=D:\\Cache\\pip`, `HF_HOME=D:\\Cache\\huggingface`, `NUGET_PACKAGES=D:\\Cache\\nuget`, `GRADLE_USER_HOME=D:\\Cache\\gradle`, `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=D:\\Cache\\ms-playwright`, `PUPPETEER_CACHE_DIR=D:\\Cache\\puppeteer`, `OLLAMA_MODELS=D:\\AI\\ollama-models`, `TEMP` and `TMP` = `D:\\Cache\\Temp`; the npm cache is `D:\\Cache\\npm` (not `D:\\npm-cache`, which was the earlier location) |\n| Junctions kept (their contents hold absolute paths) | `C:\\Users\\Anant Gupta\\miniconda3` to `D:\\Apps\\miniconda3`; `C:\\opamroot` to `D:\\Apps\\opamroot`; `AppData\\Local\\hermes` to `D:\\AI\\hermes`; `.codex\\packages` to `D:\\Cache\\codex\\packages` |\n| Watch | `Toolkit-Watch-C` (daily 08:00, read-only) wrote 7 history rows; `Toolkit-Weekly-Clean` (Sunday 03:30) **has never run** (first run 2026-10-18) |\n| Quarantines | `D:\\Backup\\Quarantine` (was `D:\\Quarantine` until 2026-10-13); its contents, including the `c-cleanup` and `c-deep-clean` review folders, were **deleted on 2026-10-17** after verification, with a context record in `D:\\Backup\\Quarantine-record\\` |\n| Suspicious files | `0K7YY.exe` and `nc6a9.exe` (identical, spoofed \"MoUsoCoreWorker\", hash mismatch) were renamed `.quarantined` and moved to the quarantine\u0027s `suspicious` folder by `01-user-cleanup.ps1`. They are absent from the profile root; that folder held 0 files when it was purged, so their final removal is not recorded. They were never run. **No full Defender scan has ever run** (last quick scan 2026-10-14) |\n\n**What did not get finished here, and its final status.**\n| Item | Final |\n|---|---|\n| Python 3.13.5 and Chocolatey | removed in Build 4; 3 visible ghost registrations cleared 2026-10-17, 7 hidden MSI component entries remain (exit 1603), invisible in Settings and `winget` |\n| .NET 8 | kept on purpose (WSL Settings needs the .NET 8 desktop runtime) |\n| Windows Ollama startup shortcut | disabled; Ollama 0.40.3 runs only when started |\n| Rize, GitKrakenCLI | removed 2026-10-17; GitLens re-downloads `gk.exe` unless GitLens is uninstalled |\n| `vscode-remote-wsl` (6.5 GiB) | **still on C:**, never decided |\n| Google DriveFS cache (4.17 GiB on C:) | decided to leave on C: |\n| Defender exclusions, BIOS battery cap (80 percent) and AC recovery | not done; need admin or the BIOS |\n| `Local\\Spotify\\Data` | 1.0 GiB (the finish script moved offline storage to `D:\\Cache\\Spotify`) |\n\n**Reading guide.** Everything below is the 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-12 audit as written. Sizes and \"pending\" markers describe that day. - `PowerShell` -- `{"command":"$h=\u0027D:\\Cache\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Anant-Gupta\\[REDACTED]\\scratchpad\\notehelp.ps1\u0027\n$add=@\u0027\n\n# Replace only the last table cell (the status) of the single row that starts with $prefix\nfunction Replace-LastCell([string]$path, [string]$prefix, [string]$newCell) {\n  $t = Read-Note $path; $lines = $t -split \"`n\"; $idx = @(); for ($i = 0; $i -lt $lines.Count; $i++) { if ($lines[$i].StartsWith($prefix)) { $idx += $i } }\n  if ($idx.Count -ne 1) { Write-Host (\"  ROW NOT UNIQUE ({0}x): {1}\" -f $idx.Count, $prefix); return $false }\n  $ln = $lines[$idx[0]].TrimEnd(); if (-not $ln.EndsWith(\u0027 |\u0027)) { Write-Host \"  row does not end with \u0027 |\u0027: $prefix\"; return $false }\n  $body = $ln.Substring(0, $ln.Length - 2); $k = $body.LastIndexOf(\u0027 | \u0027); if ($k -lt 0) { return $false }\n  $lines[$idx[0]] = $body.Substring(0, $k + 3) + $newCell + \u0027 |\u0027; Write-Note $path ($lines -join \"`n\"); return $true\n}\n\u0027@\nif(-not ([IO.File]::ReadAllText($h)).Contains(\u0027function Replace-LastCell\u0027)){ [IO.File]::AppendAllText($h,$add,(New-Object Text.UTF8Encoding($false))) }\n. $h\n$f3b=\"$ORL\\Runs\\Build 3 Windows Cleanup\\Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 3b C Drive Snapshot Structure and Watch.md\"\nPatch-Note $f3b @(,@(\u0027## 12. - `PowerShell` -- `{"command":". \u0027D:\\Cache\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Anant-Gupta\\[REDACTED]\\scratchpad\\notehelp.ps1\u0027\n$f=\"$ORL\\Runs\\Build 4 Windows Toolkit\\Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 4 Windows Toolkit Findings.md\"\n$t=Read-Note $f; if($t.Contains(\u0027## Build 4b follow-up (2026-10-17)\u0027)){ \"already appended\"; return }\n$sec=@\u0027\n\n## Build 4b follow-up (2026-10-17)\nWork done after the first Build 4b write-up above: the Build 5 leftovers, the config adoption from the Acer, the app removals, the quarantine disposal, and the `packageManager` pin pilot. All state-changing scripts were run by the user; the pilot\u0027s local commit was made by Claude.\n\n### State-changing commands and results\n| When | Who | Command | Result |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 2026-10-15 15:18 to 15:31 | user | `07-build5-leftovers.ps1 -Apply` | moved Cursor\u0027s WSL server data (`snapshots` 1.98 GiB, `anysphere.cursor-agent-worker` 1.93 GiB) and the old pnpm v10 stores (0.41 GiB) to quarantine (about 13 minutes, a cross-filesystem move); disabled `ConversationCapture-Backfill-WSL`. Refused to run while a Cursor server process existed (it had exited by then). pnpm store `v3` (1.8 GiB, last written 2026-02-22) was left because it was not approved |\n| 2026-10-17 17:55 | user | `20-apply-configs.ps1` | CHANGED=3: `bat` and `fd-ignore` (the Acer\u0027s promoted files, hashes match) and `path-order` (`~\\.local\\bin` was no longer first on the user PATH; restored, old value in `D:\\Backup\\Toolkit\\20261017-175545`) |\n| 2026-10-17 17:56 | user, elevated | `09-remove-leftover-apps.ps1 -Apply` | Rize: Squirrel uninstall returned exit -1 with no message, then its folders (3.6 MB local, 166 MB roaming) and both startup values were removed. GitKrakenCLI: `AppData\\Local\\GitKrakenCLI` (365 MB: `gk.exe` plus 8 stacked versions) and `gk` removed. Python 3.13.5: the bundle uninstaller returned 1603; `msiexec /x` removed Executables, Core Interpreter and Python Launcher (exit 0), 7 hidden component entries returned 1603 and remain; the bundle\u0027s registration key was removed; Machine PATH unchanged (ExpandString, length 379, same SHA-256 prefix before and after); `C:\\Windows\\py.exe` is gone; `winget list` shows 0 Python rows |\n| 2026-10-17 17:57 to 18:14 | user | `08-quarantine-purge.ps1 -Apply` | 8 checks passed first (surviving WSL export size and SHA-256 re-verified in 1.7 min, WSL running, Tailscale, Cursor 3.24.18, OpenClaw gone, lock present, no Cursor update in progress); deleted 11 folders (53.77 GiB) and the malformed manifest copy; **D: free 398.7 to 452.8 GiB**; context record written first |\n| 2026-10-17 | Claude | `packageManager` pin pilot (below) | one local commit in an isolated worktree; nothing pushed |\n\n### Quarantine disposal record (what existed before it was deleted)\nWritten by the purge script to `D:\\Backup\\Quarantine-record\\[REDACTED].txt`; the files themselves are gone. Nothing in it was needed to run the machine.\n| Folder | Size | What it held |\n|---|---:|---|\n| `[REDACTED]` | 4.33 GiB | Cursor server `snapshots` and agent-worker data, old pnpm v10 stores, `undo.txt` |\n| `[REDACTED]` | 2.39 GiB | the 2026-09-20 Codex cleanup quarantine (2.34 GiB, 55,449 files), five stray profile-root files (a temp copy of a Claude config, a battery report, an empty `global.yml`, `settings.json`, the mongosh installer), an empty `suspicious` folder |\n| `[REDACTED]` | 8.52 GiB | app data and installers of already-removed software: Outlook, Office and Olk caches (2.4 GiB), Medal (0.8), Kingsoft/WPS (0.7), the Brave profile (0.67; bookmarks and any saved logins), Opera (0.5), JetBrains settings (0.23), Steam, Riot, Cluely and Guardian updater caches, Respondus, Teams add-in, `.jdks`, and ten old setup files from Downloads (PowerToys, Kiro, Cursor 2.6.12, Loom, Vivaldi, Dell Command Update, Jan, Miniconda, GitHub CLI 2.89.0) |\n| `[REDACTED]` | 1.76 GiB | the install Cursor\u0027s interrupted self-update broke (33,923 files) |\n| `cursor-oldlogs`, `cursor-state-*` (4 folders) | 0.03 GiB | Cursor logs older than 7 days, Crashpad, process monitor, update supervisor, blob storage |\n| `[REDACTED]` | 0.00 GiB | the `E START App` shortcut, the `OpenClaw Gateway` cmd, `~\\.openclaw` (31 skill links), and `undo.txt` |\n| `[REDACTED]` | 36.75 GiB | the old WSL export `wsl-ubuntu.tar`: written 2026-01-15, 39,454,945,280 bytes, 455,976 entries, SHA-256 `[REDACTED]`; 21.7 GB of it was a Rust `target/` folder from `boom`. Replaced by the 2026-10-14 export |\nWhat remains outside the quarantine and was kept on purpose: `D:\\Backup\\WSL` (the 54.47 GiB export, manifest and `.sha256`), `D:\\Backup\\Toolkit` (config backups), `D:\\Backup\\[REDACTED]` (a copy of `.bashrc` with the key line blanked), `D:\\Backup\\GameSaves`.\n\n### `packageManager` pin pilot (what it is, what happened)\n**What a pin is.** A repo\u0027s `package.json` field `\"packageManager\": \"pnpm@12.4.2\"` makes Corepack run exactly that pnpm version in that repo on every machine, whatever is installed globally. On the Dell `pnpm` is a Corepack shim, so the pin is honoured. It stops the version drift that hit the Acer (pnpm floated to 12.11.2 after its weekly cleanup deleted `~/.cache/node`). With pnpm 12 the lockfile also records the pnpm version with integrity hashes. It helps pnpm repos only: npm repos are not pinned, and Python repos need `.python-version` and `uv.lock` instead.\n**Method.** One repo at a time, in a separate `git worktree` off `origin/main` on branch `dell-latitude/pin-package-manager` (so the main checkout\u0027s uncommitted files are untouched, per Decision 11), then `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and the repo\u0027s own checks.\n| Repo | Result |\n|---|---|\n| `hub/Learning-Tracker-Tool` | **Done, committed locally** as `51cc182` (\"Pin pnpm 12.4.2 through packageManager\"), unpushed, upstream unset; worktree at `~/projects/hub/.worktrees/Learning-Tracker-Tool-pin-package-manager`. Diff: `package.json` +1 line, `pnpm-lock.yaml` +158 lines (the new package-manager section), `pnpm-workspace.yaml` +3 lines. Verified: frozen install exit 0 under pnpm 12.4.2, Next.js 16.1.6 starts, eslint exit 0. It is a **team repo** (remote `Shauryya27/Learning-Tracker-Tool`, merges from a collaborator): pushing the pin forces pnpm 12.4.2 on them, and older pnpm versions have not been tested against the new lockfile section, so the push or PR is the user\u0027s decision |\n| `hub/portfolio` | **Blocked, reverted and removed.** The lockfile on `origin/main` is already broken (duplicate key `@ai-sdk/mistral@3.0.39` at lines 201 and 207), so pnpm discarded it and wanted to regenerate all 5,545 lines, which would also float dependency versions; its `pnpm.onlyBuiltDependencies` block is also ignored by pnpm 12. Needs its own decision on a branch. The main checkout (`post-frontend`, 114 uncommitted files) was never touched |\n| `hub/Assisto_website` | Not attempted: it has both `package-lock.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml`; choose one manager first |\n| npm, bun, yarn and Python repos | not pinned (see the register in the Locked Decisions note) |\n**What pnpm 12 changed (applies to every pnpm repo, with or without a pin).** The old `ignoredBuiltDependencies` / `pnpm.onlyBuiltDependencies` keys are no longer honoured; `pnpm install` fails with `ERR_PNPM_IGNORED_BUILDS` until each build script is decided in `allowBuilds` (here `sharp: false`, `unrs-resolver: false`, preserving the old intent). With a `packageManager` field present, a frozen install fails until the lockfile has been updated once to record pnpm itself (`pnpm install --lockfile-only`).\n\n### Errors and root causes (follow-up)\n| Error | Root cause | Fix |\n|---|---|---|\n| C: free fell 17.5 GiB in one day (137.8 to 120.3) with no alert | the system-managed pagefile grew from about 16 GB (3b snapshot) to 31 GB under memory use; the alert rules need a week of history | inference, to confirm after a reboot; read C: free after a reboot before suspecting data |\n| `gk.exe` keeps returning after deletion | the GitLens extension (VS Code, Kiro, the WSL VS Code server) downloads and self-updates the GitKraken CLI for its MCP feature; no documented opt-out | folders cleared; they return unless GitLens is uninstalled (user decision, extension lists sync) |\n| 7 Python registrations survive `msiexec /x` (1603) | they are hidden `SystemComponent` entries of the removed bundle; cause of the failure not established | harmless, invisible in Settings and `winget`; optional registry cleanup |\n| `pnpm store path` showed `/mnt/c/.pnpm-store/v11` | `wsl.exe --exec` starts in a Windows directory and pnpm picks its store from the current directory\u0027s filesystem | from `~` it is `~/.local/share/pnpm/store/v11`; do not run it from a `/mnt` directory |\n| Firecrawl key still accepted by the provider after the user\u0027s \"revocation\" | not established; a fake key and no key both return 401, so the check discriminates | open: revoke the most recently used key in the dashboard |\n\n### Growth rules (added in 4b and its follow-up)\n| Standing rule | Check |\n|---|---|\n| Never kill an app whose updater may be running; read its state first | the last `update#setState` line in the newest Cursor `main.log` must be `idle` before a script stops Cursor |\n| A comma list through `powershell -File` is one string; take a string parameter and split it into a new variable (a typed parameter coerces an array back) | run with `-Stages A,B` and read the first log line |\n| Native stderr can abort a PS 5.1 script that sets `$ErrorActionPreference = \u0027Stop\u0027`, even with `2\u003e$null`; and quotes inside `wsl.exe` arguments get mangled | run natives under `Continue`, or call a helper `.sh` file |\n| Parenthesize every `\u0027key=\u0027 + $v` inside an `@(a, b)` list (the comma binds tighter than `+`) | `($lines).Count` equals the number of elements |\n| Verify a WSL export with GNU tar inside WSL, record entries and SHA-256, and say no import test was run | the `.manifest.txt` has 8 lines; `08-quarantine-purge.ps1` re-hashes it |\n| A quarantine is temporary: give it a review date, keep a text record of what it held, then delete it | `D:\\Backup\\Quarantine` should be empty; the record lives in `D:\\Backup\\Quarantine-record\\` |\n| No key in any shell rc file; revoke at the provider and confirm with a status-only call | `grep -c \"API_KEY=\" ~/.bashrc` prints 0 |\n| Judge startup behaviour by process start time versus last boot, not by the Run key | compare `(Get-CimInstance Win32_OperatingSystem).LastBootUpTime` with `(Get-Process \u003cname\u003e).StartTime` |\n| Before pinning a repo\u0027s package manager, run a frozen install in an isolated worktree | `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` exit code, then the repo\u0027s lint/test |\n| After the pagefile or memory load changes, read C: free after a reboot | `Get-PSDrive C`; `Win32_PageFileUsage` |\n\u0027@\nWrite-Note $f ($t.TrimEnd() + \"`n\" + $sec)\nCheck-Note $f","description":"Append the Build 4b follow-up section to the Build 4 findings","timeout":60000}`

- `PowerShell` -- `{"command":". \u0027D:\\Cache\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Anant-Gupta\\[REDACTED]\\scratchpad\\notehelp.ps1\u0027\n$u=\"$NL\\Ubuntu - WSL.md\"\nReplace-Line $u \u0027Does WSL need SSH keys or mirrored networking for GitHub and Jarvis MCP to work?::\u0027 \u0027Does WSL need SSH keys or mirrored networking for GitHub and Jarvis MCP to work?::No SSH key: `gh auth setup-git` gives git an HTTPS credential helper that covers GitHub. But it does need **mirrored networking** for the Jarvis and The Plan MCP servers: they are Obsidian REST APIs on the Windows side (`127.0.0.1:27123` and `:27124`), and in the default NAT mode localhost forwarding only works from Windows into WSL, not from WSL out to Windows. Both the Dell and the Acer run `networkingMode=mirrored`. (An earlier version of this card said NAT was enough; the Dell\u0027\u0027s Build 1 disproved it.)\u0027 | Out-Null\nReplace-Line $u \u0027Ollama is already planned on the Windows side with models on D: - is there any reason to also install Ollama inside WSL?::\u0027 \u0027Ollama is already planned on the Windows side with models on D: - is there any reason to also install Ollama inside WSL?::No. Under mirrored networking WSL reaches the Windows-side Ollama at `http://127.0.0.1:11434`, so a second install only duplicates model files, and a WSL Ollama service would hold the same port and block the Windows server (this happened on the Dell until Build 5 disabled it).\u0027 | Out-Null\nSet-Frontmatter $u @{ updated=\u00272026-10-17\u0027 }\n(Read-Note $u) -split \"`n\" | ? { $_.StartsWith(\u0027Does WSL need SSH keys\u0027) -or $_.StartsWith(\u0027Ollama is already planned\u0027) } | % { $_.Substring(0,170) }","description":"Replace the two wrong Ubuntu - WSL flashcards"}`

# Old Laptop Rebuild - Index

## One-Line Answer
==The old Dell (Latitude 5530) was rebuilt in five numbered builds (WSL, WSL VS Code and the SSH workflow, Windows cleanup, Windows toolkit, WSL parity) plus their follow-ups, so that it matches the newer Acer and serves as the always-on code host the Acer reaches over Tailscale; all five builds are finished and documented, the Acer ran its half on 2026-10-15, and what is left is a short list of decisions, one provider-side revocation and three future builds.==

## Where things are
| Place | Holds |
|---|---|
| this folder | this Index; [[C Drive Bloat - Failure Log and Prevention Rules]]; the two future-build plans ([[Old Laptop Rebuild - C Drive AI Platforms Audit (future build)]] and [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Anant Folder Consolidation and Drive Plan (later build)]]); four asset folders |
| `Runs/` ([[Old Laptop Rebuild - Runs Index]]) | one folder per build with its findings note (`Build 1` to `Build 5`, `Build 5b Acer Visit`) |
| `Windows Toolkit/` | the Build 4 deliverable: `toolkit.json` (manifest), `toolkit.lock.json` (versions), `_scripts/` (run order and rollback in its README), `configs/`, `catalog/` (one catalog per machine), `results/` (script result JSONs) |
| `WSL Parity/` | the Build 5 deliverable: `golden/` configs (the single source for both laptops), `_scripts/`, [[WSL Toolkit Config Reference]] |
| `Acer Dotfile Snapshot/` | the Acer's real Windows and WSL configs as exported on 2026-10-11, with hashes (the "before" for parity) |
| `New Laptop/` (parent) | [[Installations]] (live inventory), [[Ubuntu - WSL]], [[New Laptop Setup]], [[Machine Toolkit - Tool Use Cases and Agent Guide]], `Codebases/` (including the Locked Decisions note), `Sync/` (a separate series) |
| `D:\WSL\ops` and `D:\WSL\ops\windows` | the live host scripts and their logs on the Dell (vault copies of the ones worth keeping are under `Windows Toolkit/_scripts/host-ops` and `WSL Parity/_scripts`) |
Retired on 2026-10-17: every build prompt, the Acer-facing handoff and run-sheet notes (their results live in the findings notes), the old Index history and all backup copies. Everything retired is recoverable from the vault's git history.

## Starting state (2026-10-04)
| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Machine | Dell Latitude 5530, i7-1255U (10 cores, 12 threads), 32 GB RAM, Intel Iris Xe only (no NVIDIA GPU), one 1 TB NVMe |
| Disks | C: 251.9 GB (28.6 GB free, 93 percent used by 2026-10-07 at 20 GiB free), D: 700 GB (about 366 GB free) |
| C: biggest | Users 128 GB (AppData 100 GB), Windows 39 GB, `pagefile.sys` 25.6 GB (a RAM-sized fallback), Program Files 26 GB |
| D: biggest | WSL 176 GB (`ext4.vhdx` 105 GB, `wsl-ubuntu.tar` 36.7 GB), Docker VHDX 33.8 GB, Games 86 GB, recycle bin 32.5 GB |
| WSL | kernel 5.15.167.4 (old), `.wslconfig` 16 GB / 8 CPUs with no swap setting (so swap landed on C:) |
| Things found that changed the plan | no discrete GPU, so heavy local-model work belongs on the Acer; the pagefile warning had a visible cause (a configured D: pagefile that never gets created); WSL swap defaults to C:; the Jarvis MCP from WSL needs mirrored networking; running `wsl --shutdown` from inside WSL kills the session |

## The builds
| Build | Dates | Layer | Result | Findings |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2026-10-04 to 10-07 | WSL | repaired in place; 41.7 GiB of regenerable data removed; `.wslconfig` retuned for an always-on host; WSL updated; 75-second idle test passed | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 1 WSL Findings]] |
| 2 | 2026-10-07 to 10-15 | WSL VS Code and the two-laptop SSH workflow | WSL-side VS Code parity; Tailscale SSH between `dell-wsl` and `gupta-builds`; host key verified; `sync-check`; port-2222 fallback and VS Code Tunnel declined | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 2 VS Code and Two-Laptop Findings]] |
| 3 (+3b) | 2026-10-11 to 10-12 | Windows C: cleanup, structure, watch | C: free 39.5 to 136 GiB; caches redirected to D:; pagefile settled; daily watcher and weekly clean registered | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 3 Windows Cleanup Findings]], [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 3b C Drive Snapshot Structure and Watch]] |
| 4 (+4b) | 2026-10-11 to 10-17 | Windows toolkit | manifest-driven toolkit, 104 PASS, lock written; catalog, agent guide, Installations; Cursor root cause; WSL export; startup, OpenClaw, Rize, quarantine cleanup; pnpm-pin pilot | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 4 Windows Toolkit Findings]] |
| 5 (+5b) | 2026-10-13 to 10-15 | WSL parity | configs equal to the Acer's, then one golden set for both; upkeep timers; Miniconda; tmux 3.8, pnpm 12.4.2; DCIM duplicates removed | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Build 5 WSL and D Drive Findings]] |
| Acer visit | 2026-10-15 | Acer side of Builds 2, 4 and 5b | golden WSL set applied, Windows toolkit Parts 0 to 3 (0 FAIL), host key MATCH, WSL updated to 3.0.3.0 | [[Old Laptop Rebuild - Acer Visit Findings (2026-10-15)]] |
| 6 | not written | Windows VS Code, MCP registry and Claude Code settings; AI platforms audit | open: Codex `node_repl` path, ~50 skill-junction dot folders, Settings Sync impact | plan: [[Old Laptop Rebuild - C Drive AI Platforms Audit (future build)]] |
| 7 | not written | dev-only profile and a final verification against the Acer | open | none |

"Build 5b" means the WSL golden-config toolkit upgrade: the Dell's part is in the Build 5 findings, the Acer's part in the Acer visit note. "Build 4b" is the Dell leftovers and Tailscale/SSH decisions inside the Build 4 findings. Earlier drafts of these notes used a different numbering (for example "Build 5 Windows VS Code" and "Build 6 final parity"); that was superseded by the table above.

## Final state (verified live on the Dell, 2026-10-17)
| Area | State |
|---|---|
| Dell Windows | Windows 11 Pro 26300; PowerShell 7.6.7 default; Git 2.55.0, GitHub CLI 2.102.0, Node 24.20.0, pnpm 12.11.2, uv 0.12.19; Terminal One Half Dark with IosevkaTerm Nerd Font; Cursor 3.24.18 (auto-update on); Windows Ollama 0.40.3 from `D:\Apps\Ollama` when started; .NET 8 kept for WSL Settings; nothing in the Run key or Startup folder starts by itself. Details: [[Installations]] |
| Dell disks | C: 120.8 GiB free (it was 145.4 on 2026-10-13; the system-managed pagefile grew to 31 GB, to be confirmed after a reboot), D: 452.8 GiB free; `D:\Backup\Quarantine` is empty |
| Dell WSL | 3.0.3.0, kernel 6.18.54.1, Ubuntu 24.04.4, systemd `running` with 0 failed units, 19 GiB / 10 CPUs / 8 GiB swap on `D:\WSL\swap.vhdx`, mirrored networking, both idle timers off; golden configs `same=34`; `wsl-clean.timer` and `wsl-watch.timer` active |
| Dell WSL backup | `D:\Backup\WSL\ubuntu-20261014-233219.tar` (54.47 GiB, 1,114,217 entries, SHA-256 in its manifest, re-hashed 2026-10-17). **Never import-tested.** The only WSL backup |
| Tailscale and SSH | `dell-wsl` (WSL, `RunSSH=true`) and `gupta-builds` (Acer Windows) connected over a direct path; Tailscale SSH only; host key verified |
| Acer (reported 2026-10-15) | golden WSL set, tmux 3.8, pnpm 12.4.2, WSL 3.0.3.0, Windows toolkit 0 FAIL; C: 166.5 GiB free; only `UV_CACHE_DIR` of the cache variables set; 4 internship commits exist only on the Acer |

## Standing decisions (locked, do not relitigate)
- **Two-laptop workflow (2026-10-04):** the Dell is the canonical host with one checkout per shared repo on WSL ext4; the Acer connects with VS Code Remote-SSH over Tailscale; GitHub is the backup and PR channel; parallel work uses git worktrees on `<machine>/<topic>` branches, one session per task; no sync daemon touches code. Reasoning and outcomes: [[Codebases - Two-Laptop SSH Workflow - Locked Decisions and Postmortem]].
- **Tailscale placement:** inside WSL on the Dell, Windows side only on the Acer; never on the Dell's Windows side, never inside the Acer's WSL.
- **Settings Sync is live.** Any Windows VS Code user file written on the Dell reaches the Acer; Windows VS Code, MCP and Claude Code settings belong to Build 6.
- **Mirrored networking stays** (the Jarvis and The Plan MCP servers are Windows-side REST APIs on `127.0.0.1:27123` and `:27124`).
- **Never `wsl --shutdown`** from inside WSL and never restart WSL from a session; host steps go into a script the user runs.
- **No discrete GPU on the Dell** (Iris Xe only); heavy local-model work belongs on the Acer (RTX 5070 Ti).
- **Layout contract (Build 3b):** C: holds Windows, installs, drivers and the profile; `D:\Apps`, `D:\Cache\<tool>`, `D:\AI`, `D:\WSL`, `D:\Backup` hold the rest; a new tool sets its environment variable first.
- **Quarantines are temporary:** a review date in the folder name, a text record of what it held, then deletion. Only regenerable caches are deleted outright. Never delete `~/.cache/node` (it holds the Corepack pin).
- **Always-on host:** `Toolkit-Weekly-Clean` and the daily `Toolkit-Watch-C` on Windows; `wsl-clean.timer` and `wsl-watch.timer` in WSL.
- **Shared versions (2026-10-14):** pnpm 12.4.2 and tmux 3.8 in WSL on both laptops; Terminal scheme One Half Dark, font IosevkaTerm Nerd Font; PowerShell 7 is the default shell; the golden WSL configs in `WSL Parity/golden/` are the single source.
- **Cursor auto-update stays on** (2026-10-14): fix causes, never force-kill Cursor while its updater runs.
- **Drive and `_Anant` (2026-10-13):** `D:\_Anant` becomes the one main folder, Jarvis and The Plan stay out of any Drive mirror, and no mirror is turned on until codebases and cache trees leave the mirrored root.

## Rules learned the hard way (full table in each findings note)
1. Read an updater's state before stopping its app (Build 4 findings).
2. `powershell -File` passes a comma list as one string; a typed parameter coerces an array back; parenthesize `'key=' + $v` inside `@( , )`; native stderr can abort a PS 5.1 script under `Stop`; quotes inside `wsl.exe` arguments get mangled, so use a helper script (Build 4 findings).
3. Check elevation with the role enum, not the string "Administrator"; test every wildcard filter in a dry run; opam and Cygwin files carry the System attribute (Build 3b).
4. Verify a WSL export with GNU tar inside WSL, record the SHA-256, and say plainly that no import test was run (Build 5 findings).
5. After a pagefile or memory change, read C: free after a reboot before suspecting data (Failure Log).
6. No secret in any shell rc file; revoke at the provider and confirm with a status-only call (Build 4 findings).
7. Judge startup behaviour by process start time versus boot time, not by the Run key (Build 4 findings).
8. Before pinning a package manager, run a frozen install in an isolated worktree (Build 4 findings).
9. Only the session that closes a build edits this Index; a Syncthing conflict file is diffed before it is discarded (the 2026-10-13 incident).

[[VS Code - Windows]] still says Docker starts at logon; it does not.

## Logging rule for every session
1. A session writes `Old Laptop Rebuild - Build N <layer> Findings.md` in `Runs/Build N .../`, with a verified outcome, a before and after table, every state-changing command and its result, the root cause of every error, and Growth rules (the standing rule and one check command). It adds one line to `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/Session Logs/log.md`.
2. **One owner per note.** Only the session that closes a build edits this Index and the Runs Index.
3. A prompt is a working document: once its build is closed it is deleted and the findings keep the facts. Quarantines and backups follow the same rule.
4. If Syncthing writes a `*.sync-conflict-*` file, diff it against the live note and merge anything unique before discarding it.

| Build 1, WSL layer | 2026-10-04 to 10-07 | 36.4 GiB of project artifacts and 5.3 GiB of caches removed inside ext4; `.wslconfig` retuned (20 GB, 10 CPUs, 8 GiB swap on D:, both idle timers off); WSL 2.4.13.0 to 3.0.1.0; 75-second idle test passed |

| Build 5, Dell WSL parity (+5b) | 2026-10-13 to 10-14 | configs equal to the Acer's real files, then one golden set; ruff, shellcheck, shfmt, Miniconda; upkeep timers; tmux 3.8 and pnpm 12.4.2; DCIM duplicates (3,799 MiB) removed; ChatGPT Classic removed |
| Acer visit | 2026-10-15 | golden WSL set applied (`changed=13 same=21 fails=0`), Windows toolkit Parts 0 to 3 (0 FAIL), Build 2 SSH checks, WSL updated to 3.0.3.0, 4 local-only internship commits found and bundled |

## Incidents worth remembering
- **2026-10-13, 15 Syncthing conflict files:** two Dell Claude sessions edited the same notes at once (one under a renamed `_Rebuild - Index`). No technical lock exists; the Index's "one owner per note" rule is the response, and Decision 11 in the Locked Decisions note records the same limit for code worktrees.
- **2026-10-14, Cursor's update broke twice:** a script force-killed it while its updater was applying an update. Fixed in the script; auto-update stays on (Build 4 findings).
- **2026-10-14, a WSL export "crashed" after writing the tar:** Windows `tar.exe` cannot read 56 pax names and exited nonzero under PS 5.1 `Stop`; the archive was sound (GNU tar listing, exit 0).
- **2026-10-17, C: free fell 17.5 GiB in a day with no alert:** most likely the system-managed pagefile growing to 31 GB; confirm after a reboot.

- `cat > /d/WSL/ops/windows/build5-leftovers.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# build5-leftovers.sh  (Build 4b, A10, 2026-10-15)  ASCII only. Helper for 07-build5-leftovers.ps1; no quoting passes through PowerShell.
#   build5-leftovers.sh check                 prints cursor_server=<n> and SIZE|<name>|<size or missing>|<path> for each item
#   build5-leftovers.sh move cursor <dest>    moves the Cursor server items (snapshots, agent-worker) into <dest> (a /mnt/d path)
#   build5-leftovers.sh move pnpm <dest>      moves the old pnpm v10 stores into <dest>
# Items (all under $HOME). pnpm v3 (1.8 GB, last written 2026-02-22) is deliberately NOT listed: it was not approved.
mode="${1:-check}"; group="${2:-}"; dest="${3:-}"
declare -A path=(
  [cursor-snapshots]="$HOME/.cursor-server/data/snapshots"
  [cursor-agent-worker]="$HOME/.cursor-server/data/User/globalStorage/anysphere.cursor-agent-worker"
  [pnpm-v10-share]="$HOME/.local/share/pnpm/store/v10"
  [pnpm-v10-home]="$HOME/.pnpm-store/v10"
)
cursor_items=(cursor-snapshots cursor-agent-worker)
pnpm_items=(pnpm-v10-share pnpm-v10-home)
if [ "$mode" = "check" ]; then
  echo "cursor_server=$(pgrep -fc '[c]ursor-server')"
  for k in "${cursor_items[@]}" "${pnpm_items[@]}"; do
    p="${path[$k]}"
    if [ -e "$p" ]; then echo "SIZE|$k|$(du -sh "$p" 2>/dev/null | cut -f1)|$p"; else echo "SIZE|$k|missing|$p"; fi
  done
  exit 0
fi
if [ "$mode" = "move" ]; then
  case "$group" in cursor) items=("${cursor_items[@]}") ;; pnpm) items=("${pnpm_items[@]}") ;; *) echo "bad group"; exit 2 ;; esac
  case "$dest" in /mnt/d/Backup/Quarantine/*) ;; *) echo "STOP: destination must be under /mnt/d/Backup/Quarantine"; exit 2 ;; esac
  mkdir -p "$dest" || exit 3
  for k in "${items[@]}"; do
    p="${path[$k]}"
    [ -e "$p" ] || { echo "MOVED|$k|absent|0"; continue; }
    mv "$p" "$dest/$k"; rc=$?
    echo "MOVED|$k|$dest/$k|$rc"
  done
  exit 0
fi
echo "usage: check | move cursor|pnpm <dest>"; exit 2
EOF
sed -i 's/\r$//' /d/WSL/ops/windows/build5-leftovers.sh; grep -c $'\r' /d/WSL/ops/windows/build5-leftovers.sh; LC_ALL=C grep -c -P '[^\x00-\x7F]' /d/WSL/ops/windows/build5-leftovers.sh; echo written`
